# Trophies

Every bug found by a test in the zoo, generated by `tools/zoo report` from the
`bugs.toml` files under `targets/`. Do not edit by hand.

2037 bugs across 286 targets: duplicate 10, fixed 45, open 1979, reported 3.

| [uv-pep508/1](targets/rust/uv-pep508/bugs.toml) | String-keyed markers are compared lexicographically even when both sides are valid versions: `platform_release < '6'` is true for release `22.6.0`; PEP 508 says PEP 440 rules apply whenever both sides are valid versions (and `packaging` says false) | wrong-result | medium | 0.0.80 (c0df400a4) | open |  |
| [uv-pep508/2](targets/rust/uv-pep508/bugs.toml) | An unsatisfiable `MarkerTree` displays as `python_version < '0'` — its own canonical spelling of `false` — but that text parses back to a satisfiable-looking tree (`python_full_version < '0'`, `is_false()` = false, not equal to `MarkerTree::FALSE`) | roundtrip | low | 0.0.80 (c0df400a4) | open |  |

| [uv-pep508/4](targets/rust/uv-pep508/bugs.toml) | `python_version` markers are wrong on pre-release interpreters: `python_version == '3.13'` is false and `python_version < '3.13'` true when `python_full_version` is `3.13.0rc1` — the expression is lowered to a release-only `python_full_version` range and tested against the environment's real pre-release value | wrong-result | high | 0.0.80 (c0df400a4) | open |  |
| [uv-pep508/5](targets/rust/uv-pep508/bugs.toml) | `python_version != '3.5.1.*'` is the tree `false` (unsatisfiable) — the complement of an unsatisfiable `==` must be `true`, as `python_version != '3.5.1'` is and as `packaging` says | wrong-result | medium | 0.0.80 (c0df400a4) | open |  |

| [versions/1](targets/rust/versions/bugs.toml) | `Versioning::parse` stops after a `SemVer`/`Version` prefix, so `Requirement::new` rejects versions `Versioning::new` accepts: `^1.2.3.4`, `<10.1.22000.832`, `=1.2.3-1.el9_4` are `None` | correctness | medium | 8.0.0 (3ae61d089) | open | [issue](https://github.com/fosskers/rs-versions/issues/31) |
| [versions/2](targets/rust/versions/bugs.toml) | `~` and `^` on general versions ignore the last component: `~1.2.3.4` matches `1.2.3.0` and `1.2.4.0`, `^1.2.3.4` matches `1.2.3.0`, `^1.2` matches `1.1`, and `~7`/`^7` match nothing — not even `7` | correctness | medium | 8.0.0 (3ae61d089) | open |  |

| [versions/4](targets/rust/versions/bugs.toml) | A `SemVer` compares `Equal` to a general version whose last part is an RC or post-letter with the same number: `Versioning::new("1.2.3") == "1.2.3rc1" == "1.2.3b" == "1.2.3-alpha"` by `cmp`, while `Version` orders `1.2.3rc1 < 1.2.3` | correctness | medium | 8.0.0 (3ae61d089) | open |  |
| [versions/5](targets/rust/versions/bugs.toml) | `Mess`'s `Ord` ignores the separator kind and the digits' spelling that its derived `Eq` includes: `Mess::new("1-2").cmp("1_2")` and `"1.01".cmp("1.1")` are `Equal` while `==` is false | correctness | low | 8.0.0 (3ae61d089) | open |  |
| [versions/6](targets/rust/versions/bugs.toml) | `Version::to_mess` builds a different `Mess` than `Mess::new` parses from the same text: an `rN` chunk becomes `Plain`, not `Rev` (so `1.2.r3 > 1.2.4_1` via `to_mess`, `<` via `Mess::new`), and a hyphenated release identifier `b-c` stays one `Plain` chunk | correctness | low | 8.0.0 (3ae61d089) | open |  |

| [agrona/1](targets/java/agrona/bugs.toml) | MutableDirectBuffer.putNaturalIntAsciiFromEnd(0, endExclusive) writes nothing and returns endExclusive: the digit loop is `while (remainder > 0)`, so zero, a natural number whose digitCount is 1 and for which the method reserves one byte with ensureCapacity, gets an empty encoding instead of "0" at endExclusive - 1 (AbstractMutableDirectBuffer.putNaturalIntAsciiFromEnd; upstream's shouldPutNaturalFromEnd test never uses 0) | wrong-result | low | 2.7.0-SNAPSHOT (2026-08-31) (6a57dd3df) | open |  |
| [agrona/2](targets/java/agrona/bugs.toml) | parseIntAscii and parseNaturalIntAscii (the DirectBuffer methods and AsciiEncoding.parseIntAscii(CharSequence)) throw AsciiNumberFormatException("int overflow parsing") for any input of more than 10 digits regardless of its value, so a decimal int with leading zeros such as 00000000001 is rejected although it is a valid representation of 1 (parseLongAscii reads the same bytes as 1); parseLongAscii does the same past 19 digits (00000000000000000001); putNaturalPaddedIntAscii(index, 11, 5) itself writes a string that parseNaturalIntAscii(index, 11) cannot read back (parsePositiveIntAsciiOverflowCheck: `if ((end - startIndex) > INT_MAX_DIGITS) throwParseIntOverflowError(...)`) | wrong-result | low | 2.7.0-SNAPSHOT (2026-08-31) (6a57dd3df) | open |  |

| [sbe/2](targets/java/sbe/bugs.toml) | IrGenerator gives the BEGIN_SET/END_SET tokens of a bit set an Encoding without the schema's byte order (Encoding.Builder default LITTLE_ENDIAN) while the CHOICE tokens carry it, so in a bigEndian schema the IR says the set is little-endian and its choices big-endian; the Go flyweight generator, which formats each token's own byte order, emits Clear()/IsEmpty() with the little-endian helpers and the choice accessors with the big-endian ones (add(SetType, ...) in xml/IrGenerator.java lacks the .byteOrder(schema.byteOrder()) that add(EnumType, ...) and add(SetType.Choice, ...) have) | wrong-result | low | 1.41.0-SNAPSHOT (2026-09-09) (89bb53644) | open |  |
| [sbe/3](targets/java/sbe/bugs.toml) | OtfMessageDecoder (and so JsonPrinter) decodes a composite field added in a later schema version from the buffer of an older message instead of treating it as not present: decodeComposite passes each member token as both field and type token, so the composite field's own sinceVersion is never compared with the acting version; on a version-0 message of a schema whose version-1 composite field lies past the old block length the printer throws IndexOutOfBoundsException (Range [12, 12 + 2) out of bounds for length 12 for a 4-byte block followed by a 4-byte composite) or prints bytes of the following groups and var data as the composite's members | crash | medium | 1.41.0-SNAPSHOT (2026-09-09) (89bb53644) | open |  |
| [sbe/4](targets/java/sbe/bugs.toml) | JavaDtoGenerator's decodeWith for a composite field added in a later schema version calls <Composite>Dto.decodeWith(decoder.<field>(), ...) unconditionally, and the generated codec's accessor returns null when the acting version is older than the field's sinceVersion, so MDto.decodeFrom(buffer, offset, blockLength, actingVersion) throws NullPointerException (Cannot invoke PairDecoder.p() because <parameter1> is null) on every older message; primitive, enum, set, group and var-data properties are guarded with decoder.actingVersion() >= ...SinceVersion() | crash | medium | 1.41.0-SNAPSHOT (2026-09-09) (89bb53644) | open |  |
